| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| What a ratio is; order matters; recipes | Armenia, Grade 6, Ch.2 §2.1 Ratio p.44, §2.2 Equality of ratios, recipes p.50; Jordan, Grade 6 Term 2, Unit 6, L1 Ratio p.46 (6 glasses of orange juice per jug); Egypt, Grade 6 Term 2, Unit 9, L1 Ratio pp.16–18 (tape diagrams, double number lines; paint mixer 6 yellow : 4 red vs 9 yellow : 6 red); Greece, Grade 6, Unit 3, chs 30–39, printed pp.75–94 (ratio, equivalent ratios, proportions, cross products, direct and inverse proportion, rule of three); Sierra Leone, JSS 1 Term 2, L46 Ratio, printed p.2; Libya, Grade 5, Unit 7, finding a ratio — 2 chocolate cakes and 1 orange cake, ratio 2:1 and 1:2, printed pp.109–130; Ghana, JICA B6 teacher manual, L57–70 ratios and proportion — banku mixing, 4 cups cornmeal, 2 cups cassava, 6 cups water (teacher evidence); South Africa, Grade 4 Book 1, ws 61 ratio (picture/example only) |
| Equivalent ratios; adding to both terms does NOT keep a ratio | Jordan, Grade 6 Term 2, Unit 6, L2 Equivalent ratios p.53; Egypt, Grade 6 Term 2, Unit 9, L2 Equivalent ratios pp.19–21 (Amany's error: adding 2 to each term of 8:12 gives 10:14, which is not equivalent; ratios equivalent to 8:12); Greece, Grade 6, Unit 3, chs 30–39, printed pp.75–94 (equivalent ratios); Sierra Leone, JSS 1 Term 2, L47 Equivalent ratios, printed p.4; Libya, Grade 5, Unit 7, equivalent ratios and comparing three quantities, printed pp.109–130; Ghana, JICA B6 teacher manual, L63 equivalent ratios — men : women 5:7 with 45 men gives 63 women (teacher evidence) |
| Simplifying ratios; sharing in a ratio; dividing a length in a ratio | Sierra Leone, JSS 1 Term 2, L48–52 Ratio of a whole, ratios and fractions/percentages/decimals, simplification, sharing in ratio, 3+ terms, dividing a length in ratio, printed pp.6–20; Ghana, JICA B6 teacher manual, L57–70 ratio in simplest form — boys : girls 7:11 with 28 boys gives 44 girls, 72 altogether (teacher evidence); Mauritania, Grade 6, unequal shares p.48; Libya, Grade 6, Unit 4, ratio and fractions, ratio and proportion — money in dinars, cement/sand mixes, fish counts, water volumes in ratio 5:2, printed pp.47–66 |
| Ratio, fractions and percent are the same comparison | Sierra Leone, JSS 1 Term 2, L48 Ratios and fractions/percentages/decimals, printed p.6; Libya, Grade 6, Unit 4, ratio and fractions, printed pp.47–66; Ghana, JICA B6 teacher manual, L67–68 relation of ratio to fractions and multiplication (teacher evidence); South Africa, Grade 7 Book 1, WS7 Ratio, WS8 Rate, printed pp.14–27 |
| Unit rate and best buy | Egypt, Grade 6 Term 2, Unit 10, L1–3 Unit rate pp.32–37 (Basma, 570 jumps in 6 minutes; best-buy popcorn); Armenia, Grade 6, Ch.2 §2.2 Equality of ratios, recipes p.50; South Africa, Grade 7 Book 1, WS8 Rate, printed pp.14–27; South Africa, Grade 9 Book 1, R7 Ratio and rate |
| Proportion: two ratios equal; cross multiplication | Armenia, Grade 6, Ch.2 §2.3 Cross multiplication p.56; Egypt, Grade 6 Term 2, Unit 9, L5–6 Proportion pp.24–30 (cross-multiplication); Greece, Grade 6, Unit 3, chs 30–39, printed pp.75–94 (proportions, cross products — "crosswise I find x"); Sierra Leone, JSS 1 Term 2, L66 Simple proportion, cross-multiplication, printed p.60 |
| Direct proportion | Egypt, Primary 6 Term 2 (Student Edition), U4 C4.2 L1, printed p. 22 |
| Finding missing values in a table by ratio | Egypt, Primary 6 Term 2 (Student Edition), U5 C5.2 L3, printed p. 38 |
| Equal ratios | Egypt, Primary 6 Term 2 (Student Edition), U5 C5.2 L4, printed p. 40 |
| Unit rates | Egypt, Primary 6 Term 2 (Student Edition), U5 C5.2 L5, printed p. 42 |
| Ratios — a : b | Egypt, Prep 1 (Grade 7) Term 1 (new curriculum), U1 L1, printed p. 2 |
| Thales' theorem | Egypt, Prep 3 (Grade 9) Term 1 (old series, 2018–2019 Student's Book), U3 L1, printed p. 25 |
| Thales' corollaries | Egypt, Prep 3 (Grade 9) Term 1 (old series, 2018–2019 Student's Book), U3 L2, printed p. 27 |
| Triangle similarity — AA, SSS, SAS | Egypt, Prep 3 (Grade 9) Term 1 (old series, 2018–2019 Student's Book), U3 L3, printed p. 32 |

| Two quantities are proportional when one is obtained from the other by multiplying by a fixed number, the coefficient of proportionality (معامل التناسب); a table of such pairs is a proportionality table, tested by checking that every ratio of corresponding entries is the same (a sugar factory's beet-to-sugar table has coefficient 0.12; a boy's age against his height is NOT proportional); the cross-multiplication rule (قاعدة الضرب التقاطعي) — in a proportion the product of the extremes equals the product of the means; completing a proportionality table from one known pair; the graph of a proportion is one straight line through the origin; simple interest as a proportion application in the unit exercises | Syria, Grade 7 student's book, printed pp. 98–102, 120–121 |
| Proportionality and its graph in both directions — plotted points of proportional data lie on one straight line with the origin, and points lying with the origin on one straight line are proportional; the coefficient of proportionality as the constant ratio between the two rows of a table; average speed (السرعة الوسطى) defined as distance ÷ time, v = d/t, read off a distance–time graph; v = d/t used in all three directions with unit handling (2 h 12 min at 65 km/h is 143 km; a jet covering 28.2 km in 2 minutes averages 235 m/s; the cyclist covering 3,484.5 km at 40.4 km/h takes 86.25 h = 86 h 15 min — the example's concluding sentence misprints this as '68 hours and 15 minutes', digits transposed; every working line on the page gives 86 h 15 min, taught corrected); converting speeds between units (12.6 km/h = 3.5 m/s); scale drawing inside the proportion exercises | Syria, Grade 8 Algebra student's book, printed pp. 85–93, 102 |
| Proportional segments: two parallel lines cut by two transversals make two triangles whose side lengths are proportional — accepted without proof here and used as the 'theorem of equal ratios' (مبرهنة النسب المتساوية) to compute unknown lengths; the midpoint theorems — the segment joining the midpoints of two sides of a triangle is parallel to the third side and equals half its length, and a line through the midpoint of one side parallel to another side bisects the third side; measuring inaccessible distances and a tower's height from similar-triangle proportions | Syria, Grade 8 Geometry student's book, printed pp. 31–38, 51 |
| A proportion a/b = c/d and its terms (extremes and means) named; the basic property a/b = c/d ⇒ ad = bc; inverting both ratios, swapping the extremes, and swapping the means all preserve the equality; adding or subtracting the denominator — if a/b = c/d then (a + b)/b = (c + d)/d and (a − b)/b = (c − d)/d, with the derived forms a/(a + b) = c/(c + d) and a/(a − b) = c/(c − d); worked segment problem AN/NB = 2/3 with AB = 100 cm giving NB = 60 cm and AN = 40 cm | Syria, Grade 9 Geometry student's book, printed pp. 3–5 |
| Ratios of two quantities and of three quantities; equivalent ratios and ratios in simplest form | Malaysia, Mathematics Form 1, Ch. 4 §4.1, printed pp. 76–79 |
| Rates, including unit rates (price per litre, items per minute), and the link between a ratio and a rate | Malaysia, Mathematics Form 1, Ch. 4 §4.2, printed pp. 81–83 |
| Proportions as equal ratios or equal rates; finding the unknown value in a proportion | Malaysia, Mathematics Form 1, Ch. 4 §4.3, printed pp. 84–86 |
| A ratio of three quantities built from two given pair ratios; mixed ratio, rate and proportion problem solving | Malaysia, Mathematics Form 1, Ch. 4 §4.4, printed pp. 87–92 |
| Direct proportion first: both quantities increase or decrease together (equal ratios 1/5 = 2/10); ratio introduced through direct proportion and simplified (3:9 = 1:3), with problems on masses and counts; direct-proportion problems by the unitary route; sharing a total in a given ratio (adults:children 4:5 with 400 adults); ratio-change problems solved with simple algebra | Rwanda, Mathematics for Rwandan Primary Schools, Pupil's Book 5, Unit 6 (§§6.1–6.3), printed pp. 103–111 |
| Finding and simplifying ratios, including ratios of quantities in different units; sharing quantities in a given ratio and finding a share or the total from one share; increasing and decreasing quantities in a given ratio; the ratio of increase and the ratio of decrease; word problems involving ratios | Rwanda, Primary 6 Mathematics, Learner's Book, Unit 6, printed pp. 102–109 |
| Inverse (indirect) proportion: more workers, fewer days, and the reverse | Rwanda, Primary 6 Mathematics, Learner's Book, Unit 6, printed pp. 109–111 |
| Ratios from two quantities in the same units; simplifying ratios, including three-term ratios a : b : c; sharing quantities using ratios; ratio in scale drawing; proportion — equal ratios, the cross-product property, and the means/extremes switching property; direct proportion by the ratio method and the unitary method; inverse proportion (workers/time, taps/tank, speed/time) | Rwanda, Senior 1 Student's Book, Unit 5, printed pp. 123–135 |
| The idea of ratio — ratio from pictures and contexts (20 boys to 25 girls in a kindergarten; lesson-period counts) | Sudan, Grade 4 Mathematics, printed p. 67 |
| Ways of writing a ratio — write the ratio of a first quantity to a second in more than one written form, both directions | Sudan, Grade 4 Mathematics, printed p. 70 |
| Antecedent and consequent — first term of a ratio is its antecedent (muqaddam), second its consequent (tālī); marks-ratio examples | Sudan, Grade 4 Mathematics, printed p. 73 |
| Ratio problems and simplest form — ratio word problems (ages 10 and 40); general exercise writes ratios in simplest form both ways | Sudan, Grade 4 Mathematics, printed p. 76 |
| Ratio — Ratio as a comparison of two quantities of the same kind, written three ways (إلى wording, : symbol, fraction), antecedent and consequent named, no unit carried; ratios reduced to lowest terms; mixed-unit ratios unified first (3 km : 60 m) | Sudan, Grade 5 Mathematics, printed p. 87 |
| Equivalent ratios — Equivalent ratios by multiplying or dividing both terms; comparing two fractional ratios two ways — dividing them, and unifying denominators by LCM | Sudan, Grade 5 Mathematics, printed p. 90 |
| Proportion — Proportion defined as two equal ratios; the cross-multiplication property (product of extremes = product of means); the eight rearranged forms of one proportion; finding the missing term by equivalence and by cross-multiplication; testing whether two ratios form a proportion | Sudan, Grade 5 Mathematics, printed p. 94 |
| Ratio word problems — Ratio word problems (worked examples include the plot with length : width = 6 : 5 and length 60 m giving width 50 m on printed p. 98, and Amin's age : his father's age = 2 : 9 with the father 27 giving 6 years on printed p. 99) | Sudan, Grade 5 Mathematics, printed p. 98 |
| Proportional division — Proportional division: splitting money, tree counts and lengths in a given ratio (the lesson opens with 160 pounds split 4 : 5 : 7 → 40/50/70); Proportional division with fractional ratios first cleared to whole-number ratios (440 pounds split 4½ : 3½ : 3 → 180/140/120; tree counts split 1 : 2 : 3 → 600/1200/1800) | Sudan, Grade 5 Mathematics, printed pp. 104–105 |
| Ratio in algebraic problems — two integers in a given ratio; ratio after adding/subtracting a constant from both terms, solved with equations | Sudan, Grade 8 Mathematics (Second Year Intermediate), printed p. 30 |
| Proportion and its terms — extremes and means, first–fourth proportionals; finding third and fourth proportionals incl. algebraic quantities | Sudan, Grade 8 Mathematics (Second Year Intermediate), printed p. 36 |
| Properties of proportion — cross-product property; componendo/dividendo family (a+b)/(a−b) = (c+d)/(c−d); finding a ratio from a given equation | Sudan, Grade 8 Mathematics (Second Year Intermediate), printed p. 42 |
| Continued proportion — mean proportional: b² = ac, b = ±√(ac) | Sudan, Grade 8 Mathematics (Second Year Intermediate), printed p. 44 |
| Rate and unit rate — rate from pulse counting (140 beats / 2 min); common rates table; unit price per metre; pupils per teacher; best-buy by price per millilitre | Sudan, Grade 8 Mathematics (Second Year Intermediate), printed p. 45 |
| Ratio as comparison by division; quantities must be in the same unit first (crocodile 400 cm : lizard 20 cm = 20 : 1, example begins p. 245, computation completes p. 246) | India, Mathematics Class 6, printed p. 245 |

| Equivalent ratios by multiplying/dividing both terms; ratio in lowest form (50 : 15 = 10 : 3) | India, Mathematics Class 6, printed p. 249 |
| Proportion: two equal ratios; extreme and middle terms; checking whether ratios form a proportion | India, Mathematics Class 6, printed p. 254 |
| Ratio review — Ratio as relative comparison (150 : 75 = 2 : 1); equivalent ratios and proportion in scale problems (map: 1000 km from 2.5 cm) | India, Mathematics Class 7, printed p. 153 |
| Dividing in a ratio — Dividing ₹250 in the ratio 2 : 3 : 5 and stating each share as a percentage | India, Mathematics Class 7, printed p. 166 |
| Ratio recalled — ratio and percentage recalled and linked (part/whole as fraction, ratio and per cent) | India, Mathematics Class 8, printed p. 117 |
| Ratio: comparing two quantities — ratio (Харьцаа): an activity: discuss the following pattern and draw the 7th, 8th and 9th shapes (a. in every 3 shapes 1 is a red shape and 2 are blue shapes, and their ratio is denoted 1 : 2 — the tree diagram: 3 shapes split into 1 red and 2 blue, 6 shapes into 2 red and 4 blue, 9 shapes into 3 red and 6 blue; b. in this ratio, of 6 shapes □ are red and □ are blue; c. in this ratio, draw □ red shapes and □ blue shapes; a girl's note: "Of all the shapes, 1/3 is red and 2/3 is blue. The red shapes are two times fewer than the blue shapes"; a boy's note: "In every 3 shapes 1 is red and 2 are blue: comparing the blue shapes with the red shapes, the ratio is 2 : 1"); write the ratio fitting the following pictures (the four dot patterns, the last one worked: 3 : 3 = 1 : 1); observe the ratio and discuss it (the seven stars in two colours, with the splitting 7 = 2 + 5, then 14 = □ + □ at ×2 and 21 = □ + □: a. of the 7 shapes □ are blue and □ are red; b. the ratio of the blue shapes compared with the red shapes is 2 : 5; c. – e. the ratios of the red to the blue, of the blue to all the shapes (2 : 7) and of the red to all the shapes (5 : 7), and how many blue and red there would be among 14 shapes and among 21 shapes); this spring 15 of Dorj's small livestock gave birth: if of every 5 newborn animals 3 are lambs and 2 are kids (young goats), how many lambs and how many kids were there (the bar models of the 15 in groups of 5, and the ×3 table: 9 lambs and 6 kids); a. if one box holds 4 drinks, how many drinks are there in 12 boxes?; b. if 600 g of flour goes into one loaf, what measure of flour goes into 5 loaves?; what fraction of the state flag is red and what fraction is blue (the pictured Mongolian flag with its red-blue-red bands: red is 2/3 and blue is 1/3); comparing the red part with the blue part: □ : □; comparing the blue part with the red part: □ : □; comparing the blue part with the whole part: □ : □; observe the shape's parts and write the ratios (the table on the pictured three-band rectangle: the blue part compared with the whole part is 1 : 3 (given); the blue part compared with the yellow part □ : □; the yellow part compared with the whole part □ : □; the yellow part compared with the blue part is 2 : 1 (given)); a. express the shaded part as a common fraction; b. write the ratio of the shaded part compared with the whole part (the two bar models: the first, with 1 of 4 parts shaded, worked as the fraction 1/4 and the ratio 1 : 4; the second, with 2 of 6 parts shaded, to fill in); two brothers shared 15 gingerbreads in the ratio 2 : 3: how many did each brother take (the bar models of the elder brother's 2 parts and the younger brother's 3 parts: the total is 2 + 3 = 5 parts; 15 ÷ 5 = 3 gingerbreads in one part; the elder brother took 3 × 2 = 6 and the younger 3 × 3 = 9); if two children together plant one tree, how many trees must a class of 32 children plant (16; the pairing diagram of the children and the trees); observe the ratio tables and compose problems (a. the ÷ 4 table: 24 → 6 and 8 → □; b. the × 5 table: 10 → □ and 12 → 60; c. the table 120 → 30 and □ → 15); a school has 140 flowers in all: 1/7 of them are one kind and the rest are sarnai (rose) flowers: write the ratio comparing the first kind's count with the sarnai flowers' count (20 : 120, that is 1 : 6) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 77–78 |
| Ratio problems: mixtures, sharing, scaling recipes — observe which part is compared with which part and discuss (the bar with one of its five parts shaded green: a. express the shaded part as a common fraction □/□; b. write the ratio of the shaded part compared with the whole part □ : □; c. express the unshaded part as a common fraction □/□; d. write the ratio of the shaded part compared with the unshaded part □ : □); the ratio of a father's and a son's heights is 2 : 1, and the father's height is 174 cm: find the son's height (87 cm); yellow and blue paints were mixed in the ratio 1 : 2: how will the colour change if the yellow and blue paints are mixed in the ratio 1 : 3 or 1 : 1 — experiment and see; to bake a loaf, 5 g of salt and 15 g of granulated sugar go into 100 g of flour: write the ratio of the flour, salt and sugar in one loaf (100 : 5 : 15, that is 20 : 1 : 3); a teacher wrote 2 stars and 1 piece of advice for each pupil: for the class's 30 pupils, how many stars and how many pieces of advice did she write in all (60 stars and 30 pieces of advice, the pictured star and envelope icons); in a bag, the red apples are 3 times more than the yellow apples: if 8 apples are taken into one bag, how many red apples and how many yellow apples are taken into 12 bags (in each bag 6 red and 2 yellow, so 72 red and 24 yellow); a shop received 121 boxes of shirts: in one box there were packed 2 yellow, 3 blue and 5 red shirts: how many yellow, blue and red shirts did the shop receive (242, 363 and 605); a herder has 180 head of sheep and cattle together: the cattle are 5 times fewer than the sheep: find the numbers of sheep and cattle and write the ratio (150 sheep and 30 cattle, shown by the bar models; the ratio 5 : 1); 10 mm of red ribbon accompanies each 1 cm of yellow ribbon: complete the following table (the yellow ribbon in cm: 3, □, 8, □; the red ribbon in mm: □, 40, □, 10 — equal lengths in the two units); when grandmother brews tea she puts the water and the milk in the ratio 2 : 1: if the ratio of the water and the milk becomes 3 : 1 or 1 : 1, discuss how the tea's colour and taste will change | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 79 |
| Equal ratios (Тэнцүү харьцаа): a boy pastes yellow and blue shapes in the ratio 1 : 2 and a girl pastes them in the ratio 2 : 4: they each paste 12 shapes: in each case, how many yellow and how many blue shapes were pasted (the bar models of the 1 : 2 bar of 3 parts and the 2 : 4 bar of 6 parts; a boy's note: "The children each pasted □ yellow and □ blue shapes. Here the ratios 1 : 2 and 2 : 4 look the same (equal)"); observe the pictures and write the equal ratios (the three subdivided bar strips); if a cook put onion and pepper into the food in the ratio 1 : 3, write each row in the table in equal ratios (the table: 1 : 3, 2 : 6, □ : 9, 4 : 12, 5 : 15); for the other tables, compose problems that fit them and complete the equal ratios (the tables of shape pairs, of apples and mushrooms (3 : 2, 6 : 4, □ : 6, 12 : 8, 15 : 10), and of apples and strawberries); a boy diluted one cup of pure sea-buckthorn juice with 3 cups of boiled water: with this ratio, how many cups of pure sea-buckthorn juice are needed for 12 cups of boiled water (4 cups); a basketball team has 5 players: in how many teams can 35 children play (7); in the multiplication table, write the ratio of a number in one row to the corresponding number in another row, column by column (the pictured times table with its circled pairs; a boy's note: "1/2 = 2/4 = 3/6 = 4/8 = 5/10 … — in this way the fractions are equal"; a girl's note: "Equal fractions, or equal quotients, are called equal ratios"); the box: "An equal ratio is called an equality of ratios, or a proportion (пропорц): 1 : 2 = 2 : 4 is denoted so, or written in the common-fraction form 1/2 = 2/4. The numbers that form a proportion are called the proportion's terms (гишүүд)"; let's find the proportion's unknown term (2/5 = □/30, with the cross arrows: seeing that 2 : 5 = 12 : 30, compute 30 × 2 ÷ 5 = □; the labels: the product of the extreme terms (Захин гишүүдийн үржвэр) equals the product of the middle terms (Дундах гишүүдийн үржвэр)); find the unknown terms of the following equal ratios (1 : 5 = □ : 15; 18 : 54 = 1 : □; □ : 3 = 12 : 9; 7 : 5 = 28 : □; 6 : □ = 3 : 12; 13 : □ = 1300 : 800); the children drew flowers and leaves in the following pattern in the ratio 2 : 3: how many flowers and how many leaves did they draw (the flower-and-leaf sequence with its two empty boxes)? And if the flowers and leaves are drawn in the ratio 3 : 2, how many flowers and how many leaves would be drawn? Are the two ratios 2 : 3 and 3 : 2 a proportion?; you are advised to plant the spider plant in planting soil of black soil and sand mixed in the ratio 2 : 1: for 10 scoops of black soil, how many scoops of sand are needed (5); in the basketball competition, find how many balls each team scored from the teams' ratio of points; one bouquet of sarnai has red and white sarnai in the ratio 2 : 3: in a bouquet of 25 flowers, how many red sarnai and how many white sarnai are there (10 and 15); a self-check: observe the fractions' pattern and, continuing it, write 3 more numbers: check whether the compared fractions form a proportion (the halving sequence 1/3, 1/6, 1/12, 1/24, □/□, □/□, □/□) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 80–81 |
| Work rate: productivity, total work, time — an elder brother raced his younger brother at reading by the minute: the younger brother read 120 words in 2 minutes: how many words will he read in 5 minutes (the diagram: the number line 0, 60, 120, ?, ?, ? with the ÷ 2 hops, and the table: 2 minutes against 120 words, halved to 1 minute; the order of solving the problem: how many words did the younger brother read in 1 minute? how many in 5 minutes?; a girl's note: "The amount of work carried out in one unit of time is called productivity (бүтээмж)"); a seamstress sews 36 gloves in 3 hours: find the productivity of the sewing in 1 hour (12 gloves), and how many gloves will the seamstress sew in 12 hours (144) (a boy's formula box: Total work = Productivity × Time; Productivity = Total work ÷ Time; Time = Total work ÷ Productivity); a mother and her two children raced at reading by the minute together: in 2 minutes the elder brother read 228 words and the younger 140 words: how many words did the elder and younger brothers read together in 5 minutes (the table with the rows time, productivity and total for the elder brother, the younger brother and both together; the order of solving: the younger's words in 1 minute; the elder's words in 1 minute; together in 1 minute; together in 5 minutes — 184 words a minute, so 920 words); a painter paints 12 chairs in an hour: in how many hours will he paint 108 chairs (9 hours); compose problems and complete the tables (a. the total-price table: the total 191828 with the unit price 1270 and the count 23; the total 56000 with the unit price 700 and the count to find; b. the total-work table: the total work 2700 with the productivity 45 and the time to find; the productivity 10 with the time 17; the time 8); a self-check: 1. two friends read the same book of 360 pages at the same time: the boy reads 8 pages a day and the girl reads 9 pages a day: who will finish, and how many days earlier (the girl, in 40 days against 45 — 5 days earlier)?; 2. using the ratio table, compose a problem and write it; let's consolidate our knowledge by playing a game (Тоглоом тоглож мэдлэгээ бататгаарай): 2-4 pupils share the cards out equally and place one on the table: the card you took is turned over in turn, and the one without a mistake can win: copy the book's page and cut along the broken lines to prepare the cards beforehand (the pictured matching cards pair a fraction-of-all-shapes statement with a ratio statement: "3/4 of all the shapes is black" with "the ratio of the black and white shapes is 3 : 1"; "2/3 of all the shapes is black" with 2 : 1; "1/3 of all the shapes is white" with "the ratio of the white and black shapes is 1 : 2"; "1/2 of all the shapes is black" with 1 : 1; "3/5 of all the shapes is black" with the black-to-white ratio 3 : 2); let's consolidate our knowledge and complete the word web (Мэдлэгээ бататган, үгийн сүлжээ нөхөөрэй) — the crossword with its eleven clues (among them: a dice-shaped solid body; the number written when a quantity is divided into parts; the length of a shape's frame (its perimeter); a line with no beginning and no end; the number of unit squares in a shape (its area); the part between two rays starting from one point (an angle); the disc's boundary (its circle); a line with a beginning and no end (a ray); a shape surrounded by a circle; a straight line with a beginning and an end (a segment)) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 82–83 |

**Acceptance count: taught in 68 countries (Kazakhstan, Jordan, South Africa, Egypt, Greece, Armenia, Sierra Leone, Mauritania, Libya, Vietnam, Ghana, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Bangladesh, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, North Macedonia, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Malaysia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Bolivia, Tajikistan, El Salvador, Cyprus, Sri Lanka, Colombia, Angola, Moldova, Belarus, Australia, Brazil, Bhutan, Costa Rica, Cuba, Eritrea, Chile, Dominica, Dominican Republic, Gabon, Ireland, Fiji, Honduras, Italy, Iceland, Mozambique, Peru, Suriname, Papua New Guinea, Timor-Leste, Uganda, Samoa).**.** Nepal carries ratio and proportion only in part at first — the unitary method (unit price × number) in Grade 3 (L24, pp. 100–101), Grade 4 (L4 §4.4), Grade 5 (Unit 14, pp. 102–105) and Grade 6 (पाठ 8, pp. 98–105), with no ratio/proportion as a topic in Grades 4–6 — then genuine ratio and proportion in Grade 7 (Unit 17, pp. 144–151: simplifying ratios, sharing in a ratio, proportion, fourth proportional) and Grade 8 (Unit 15, pp. 119–128), with direct and inverse variation in both grades' unitary units. Bangladesh carries ratio and proportion only in part — ratio and equivalent ratios in Class 6 (Ch. 5, pp. 103–137), with the unitary method (Class 5 §3.3, pp. 17–20; Class 6 Ch. 5) carrying direct proportion and unit value in substance, and ratio and proportion in Class 7 (Exp. 4, pp. 80–103) — simple, light, heavy, unit and reciprocal ratios, multi-number ratio, application of ratio, ratio and percentages, equivalent ratio, and making the national flag as a ratio of three numbers. Correction (2026-11-08 re-verification sweep): proportion equations solved by cross-multiplication (extremes × extremes = middles × middles, Exp. 4, printed pp. 100–102), sharing in a ratio (500 taka divided in the ratio 2:3, printed p. 85) and equivalent ratios and scale, incl. the National Flag's 10 : 6 construction ratios (printed pp. 92–99), ARE taught in Class 7 Exp. 4 and are counted as taught here — an earlier version of this paragraph wrongly listed them as not pinned. Best buy and working together remain unpinned. Iraq carries ratio and proportion from ratio and rate in Grade 6 (3:20; 60 km in 3 hours → unit rate 20 km/h), through proportion, solving proportions and scale drawing (Ch. 5 L3–L5), to direct and inverse proportion in Grade 7 (Ch. 2 L2-5, p. 49), proportion and measurement in triangles (Thales) in Grade 9 (Ch. 5 L5-3, p. 120), and variation — direct y = kx, inverse y = k/x, joint — in Grade 10 Literary (Ch. 1, 1-5). Armenia carries the fullest ratio Iran introduces ratio in Grade 5 and uses ratio and proportion in Grade 6.
course — ratio, equality of ratios and recipes, cross
multiplication, direct proportion, map and scale, working
together; Jordan teaches ratio and equivalent ratios in Grade 6;
Egypt teaches ratio, equivalent ratios, proportion with
cross-multiplication, unit rate and best buy in Grade 6 Term 2 —
with direct proportion, table values by ratio, equal ratios and
unit rates in the Primary 6 Term 2 book, ratio from the first
lesson of Prep 1's new curriculum, and Thales' theorem with
triangle similarity in Prep 3's old series (rows completed by
the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep);
Greece teaches ratio, equivalent ratios, proportions, cross
products, direct and inverse proportion, the rule of three, and
scale as a ratio in Grade 6; Sierra Leone teaches ratio,
equivalent ratios, ratio of a whole, simplification, sharing,
three or more terms, dividing a length in ratio, and simple
proportion in JSS 1 Term 2; Mauritania teaches proportion in
Grade 5 and proportionality with uniform motion, map scale and
unequal shares in Grade 6; Libya teaches finding a ratio,
equivalent ratios and comparing three quantities in Grade 5,
and ratio with fractions, proportion, money, mixes and counts
in Grade 6; Vietnam teaches ratio, map scale and finding two
numbers from sum and ratio / difference and ratio in Grade 5,
with no ratio beyond percent in Grade 6; Ghana teaches ratio
and proportion only as teacher-manual evidence (B6's banku
mixing, equivalent ratios, simplest form, sharing and the
relation to fractions); South Africa teaches ratio as a picture
example in Grade 4, ratio tables in Grades 5–6, ratio and rate
in Grade 7, and ratio and rate again in Grade 9; Kazakhstan
teaches proportional division in Grade 4 and joint work in
Grade 5, but no ratio or proportion as such — stated honestly
above. Laos carries ratio and proportion from proportional tables in Grade 4, through direct and inverse proportion in Grade 5, to ratio, coefficient k, the fourth proportional by cross products and inverse proportion in Grades 6–7. Yemen carries ratio and proportion from ratio in Grade 6 through proportion, enlargement, scale drawing and proportional division (including inheritance), to Thales' theorem and similarity in Grade 8. Indonesia carries ratio in full — the concept, simplest form, equivalent ratios, unit ratio and part-to-part/part-to-whole ratios in Grade 6, with scale factor, map scale and unit rate in Grade 7 Merdeka — but teaches no proportion equations or cross-multiplication anywhere in the set. Nicaragua carries ratio and proportion from multiplicative comparison in Grade 5, through ratio, equivalent ratios and proportion with the rule of three in Grade 6, to direct and inverse proportion as functions y = ax and y = a/x in the Grade 7 student book, the full rule-of-three and proportional-sharing treatment in the Grade 7 module (module evidence), and ratio between segments on the number line in Grade 9. Ethiopia carries ratio and proportion from direct and inverse proportionality in Grade 6, through ratio (including three-term ratios), proportion and direct and inverse proportionality in Grade 7, to ratio, rate, proportion and percentage in business in Grade 12 (business context only). South Sudan carries ratio and proportion from ratio as comparison with scaling in Grade 4 (3:1 flour:water; 4:5 = 8:10), through ratio/proportion by the unitary method in Grade 6 (2 learners → 10 books, so 5 → 25), ratios and proportions by the unitary method in Grade 7 (12 tins = 30 kg → 5 tins = 12.5 kg), to proportion in Secondary 1 Unit 1. Honest limit: Grade 4 p. 23 asks for unshaded:shaded but gives the fraction 2/6 (shaded:unshaded); the correct unshaded:shaded ratio is 6:2. Cambodia carries ratio and proportion whole from ratios as fractions in Grade 5 through direct and inverse proportion in Grade 8 and the proportion rule with unit conversions in Grade 9. Kuwait carries ratio and proportion from simple form in Grade 5, through equivalent ratios, direct proportion and inheritance-share problems in Grade 6, to direct and inverse proportion in Grade 8. Band C adds proportion in full in Grade 10 Term 1 — cross-multiplication, the proportion properties, continued proportion and the geometric mean. North Macedonia carries ratio and proportion in Grade 8 (2010 edition) — ratios of segments and of numbers, proportion and its unknown term, the geometric mean, and dividing a segment in a given ratio.

China carries ratio and proportion whole in Grade 6 Volume 2 — proportions and their basic property, direct and inverse proportion, scale, and enlargement and reduction — with the golden ratio and proportional segments in Grade 9.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries ratio and proportion from the Class 5 unitary-method problems through Classes 6–8's ratio, proportion and variation to Class 9's proportionality theorems and Class 10's variations unit.

Syria introduces ratio and proportion in Grade 6, formalises proportionality in Grade 7 — coefficient, tables tested ratio by ratio, cross-multiplication, the straight-line-through-the-origin graph — applies it to average speed and scale drawing in Grade 8 Algebra and to proportional segments and the midpoint theorems in Grade 8 Geometry, and finishes with the algebra of proportions (inverting, swapping extremes and means, componendo forms) in Grade 9 Geometry.

Malaysia gives ratio, rate and proportion a full Form 1 chapter — two- and three-quantity ratios, unit rates, proportions, and pair ratios combined into three-quantity ratios — before percentages join the same chapter.

Rwanda teaches ratio and proportion from Year 5 — direct proportion first, then ratio and simplifying, sharing in a given ratio and ratio-change problems — through Year 6's sharing, increasing and decreasing quantities in a ratio, and inverse proportion, to Senior 1's ratios including three-term ratios, sharing, scale drawing, and proportion with the cross-product property in both its direct and inverse forms.

Sudan builds ratio from Grade 4 (antecedent and consequent, simplest form), proportion and proportional division in Grade 5 — with fractional ratios cleared to whole numbers first — and the algebraic theory in Grade 8: proportion's terms and properties, the componendo family, continued proportion and unit rate.

India stocks ratio and proportion from Mathematics Classes 6–8: ratio as comparison by division, with quantities first put into the same unit; ratio in sharing; and direct and inverse proportion close by in Class 8's variation chapter stocking its own lesson.


Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик IV, V, VI, VII, VIII, IX (Grades 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9): ratio: comparing two quantities; ratio problems: mixtures, sharing, scaling recipes; equal ratios and proportion.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *La Patria Buena* (Grade 5), *Hecho en Venezuela* (Grade 6), *Conciencia Matemática* (Grade 8), *La Matemática de la Belleza* (Grade 9) and *Naturaleza Matemática* (Grade 10). From its books: from Grade 5: proportion — Magnitudes and proportion defined: a ratio between two magnitudes (10 km/l in town, 15 km/l outside; 289,04 kWh/month); from Grade 8: definition — magnitude, quantity and proportion: a proportion is a relation between two magnitudes; from Grade 10: direct proportionality tested and rejected for population growth (doubling time does not double the increase).

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 6: ratio; inverse ratios; dividing a quantity in a given ratio; proportion: extremes and means; finding an unknown term; from Grade 8: ratio; dividing a quantity into proportional parts; proportion and the properties of proportion; chapter key points and exercises (financial calculations).

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 5 (teacher's book): Ratio and proportion (first introduction). from Grade 6 (teacher's book): Simple ratio problems, simplifying (plans 21–22). from Grade 8 (teacher's book): .4 Ratio and proportion with decimal numbers: sharing 7 g in ratio 95 : 3.5 : 1.5 (worked: 6.65 g / 0.245 g /…; Gears and unit ratios; ratio comparisons (worked answers 2.5 : 1; ≈204 times) (plan 52); .4 STEM: direct-proportion problems (term-plan table only; no separate plan page); .2 Equivalent ratios (plan 126). from Grade 9 (teacher's book): Converting between fractions, decimals and percentages; comparing ratios; writing a fraction as a decimal (π…; Comparing ratios with a calculator; percentage as a comparison. from Grade 10 (teacher's book): Ratios in the form 1 : n and n : 1; unit conversions (coins/units) inside ratio problems. from Grade 11 (teacher's book): Ratio and proportion incl. inverse proportion (3 workers × 2 days worked in plan 13's guidance; unit price 98…; Algebraic fractions introduced; Simplifying algebraic fractions; More algebraic fractions (operations).

Bolivia enters through the Área Matemática sections of its Ministerio de Educación (Estado Plurinacional de Bolivia) Textos de Aprendizaje — the student books for Grades 7, 8, 10 and 12, each the maths section of that year's integrated year book, keeping the whole book's printed foliation, with no imprint or ISBN printed in any of them — and through the Compendio para Maestras y Maestros 2023–2024, the ministry's teacher compilation of the official student texts for all six Secundaria years (Grades 7–12), cited as teacher-compilation evidence only, never as or in place of a pupil text; its Secundaria 3 and Secundaria 5 sections are the only in-hand source for Grades 9 and 11, whose student texts are not in the record. From its books: from Grade 7: Representación gráfica de fenómenos; Razones; Proporciones; Magnitudes directamente proporcionales; from the Compendium's Secundaria 1 section (Grade 7): Razones, proporciones y regla de tres.

Tajikistan enters through its Ministry-approved pupil's textbooks in Tajik (Cyrillic), all published in Dushanbe — Математика for Grade 5 (Маориф, 2022) and Grade 6 (5th edition, Маориф, 2021), Алгебра for Grades 7 (4th improved edition, 2023) and 8 (4th edition, 2023), and Геометрия — the continuation of stereometry — for Grade 11 (3rd edition, Лавҳ, 2020). From its books: from Grade 6: Ratio of two numbers or of two quantities (after reducing to one unit); Proportion as an equality of two ratios; Solving problems by proportions (incl. shadow/similarity and dividing in a given ratio); Historical note: ratios in antiquity.

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its Class 3 syllabus requires the concept of ratio and proportion, ratios as fractions, and the simplification of ratios — ratios in their simplest form and sharing by ratio — all beginning at Class 3; Class 4 maintains the same programme, with problems involving ratios and proportions. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 14 teacher-evidence items from 10 of the guides (Book 6, Book 7, Book 10, Book 23, Book 24, Book 26, Book 27, Book 28, Book 29, Book 30; Grades 3, 4, 6, 7, 8). Proportionality runs in the guides from Grade 3 tables to percentage, average speed, scale, density and interest in Grades 5–6 — evidenced across this lesson and its neighbours (percentages, speed-distance-and-time, scale-drawing-and-maps, business-mathematics).

**Book 6:** *Al Moufid, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 1ère année du cycle secondaire collégial* (1AC = Grade 7, BIOF) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 3,742,780 bytes, 173 PDF pages. Chapters 1–2 are method framework (excluded in the placements); items are the distinct topics of the 1AC programme distribution (printed p. 73). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 7:** *Al Moufid, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 2ème année du cycle secondaire collégial* (2AC = Grade 8, BIOF) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 808,839 bytes, 93 PDF pages. ## El Salvador added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

Into this lesson the placements stock 6 items (4 from student books, 2 teacher evidence) from Book S7, Book S8, Book T7, Book S9.

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs in two passes — **94 files / 66 distinct works: 26 pupil textbooks and 40 teacher guides, all Sri Lanka Educational Publications Department (EPD) / National Institute of Education (NIE), in the country's three media — English, Sinhala and Tamil** — and placed 479 items: 289 from the pupil books and 190 teacher-evidence items (the secondary guides at one item per term of the guide's own lesson sequence; the primary guides at one item per syllabus competency group or per part). Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). The placements' named exclusions — revision exercises, answers, glossaries, assessment apparatus, and the guides' specimen lesson plans and school-based assessment instruments beyond their sequences — are stocked nowhere. Sri Lanka's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (94/94 files) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

## Colombia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Colombia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **27 distinct works: 26 student books and 1 teacher guide, all Colombia Ministerio de Educación Nacional (MEN), in Spanish** — Escuela Nueva Matemáticas cartillas Grades 1–5 (11), Nivelemos Matemáticas student workbooks Grades 1–4 (4), Aulas sin fronteras Grade 6 Unidad 1 student book and its teacher guide (2), Postprimaria student guides Grades 6–9 (4), Secundaria Activa student modules Grades 6–9 (4), and Vamos a Aprender Matemáticas 10 and 11 (2) — 3,928 PDF pages — and placed 293 teaching items: 288 from the student books and 5 teacher-evidence items from the one teacher guide (Book 23). SET CORRECTIONS carried exactly: (1) the expansion map's “28 student + 1 teacher = 29” over-counts by 2 — textbooks.tsv carries duplicate records of Escuela Nueva Grado 1 Cartilla 1 and Grado 2 Cartilla 1 (old blob/main rows for the same two works that also landed as release assets); the distinct set is 27 works and the duplicates are cited once (the library records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records); (2) the set in hand contains NO Grade 3 Cartilla 3 — Grade 3 holds Cartillas 1–2 plus the Nivelemos Grade 3 workbook, and Cartilla 2 completes Unidades 6–9; nothing in hand is a mislabelled Cartilla 3, and no never-published ruling is needed or made; (3) Nivelemos Matemáticas Grado 1 (Book 10) has NO asset in any GitHub release — it was read from the MEN source file itself, landed at exactly the census size (5,020,415 bytes) with EOF intact; the library side still owes that file a GitHub landing. Colombia COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-book rows; Book 23's rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guide companions Book 15 with teaching plans, activity scripts and answers and teaches no student content itself — cited as the evidence kind it is and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). ## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them official Escola Andorrana curriculum programmes published by decree in the Butlletí Oficial del Principat d'Andorra (BOPA); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 679 curriculum-requirement items. A programme states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Andorra item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a curriculum requirement, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is; Andorra is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Andorran pupil books are in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded 3 books in hand; the true set is 4 — the grades 1–6 programme (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) is held in the GitHub repo but sits in no staging books.json, which is why the map missed it. All four programmes are Andorra's OWN (Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan); the Spanish and French systems also taught in Andorra (Spain's Anaya and Innovamat manuals; France's Le Livre Scolaire) are list-only names on official school book lists — no file is held, so they are cited nowhere in this course and credited to no country — and the Escola Andorrana itself has no commercial pupil-textbook series, so programmes are all that exist to hold. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct stated requirement — each specific competency, and each distinct entry in the programmes' learning-resource tables, tagged by cycle where the table splits by cycle — and a procedure entry that merely restates a concept entry is folded into it, not double-counted. The placements' named exclusions — the decree preambles, the introductions and competency rationale, the didactic orientations, the assessment criteria and end-of-cycle expectations, the synthesis tables (verified verbatim repeats of the competency sections), the attitudes/values lists, Book 1's glossary and opening summary table, and the Council of Europe cross-references — are stocked nowhere. Book 3's synthesis table words its C2 end-of-cycle expectation as modelling "situacions cientificotècniques" (scientific-technical) inside the social-sciences programme — a copy-over slip in the decree as published; Book 3's competency section itself is consistently social-sciences, and the slip is copied into no row below. Book 2 states its cone/polyhedron sections twice (forms table and models table, printed pp. 32–33) — counted once in the placements and stocked once. Printed page = PDF page in all four books (Books 2–4 carry the BOPA folio; Book 1's Portal PDF is paginated at the foot the same way). Into this lesson the placements stock 8 teacher-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3.

**Book 1:** *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 1–6) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 376/2021, Annex VII; ISBN 978-99920-0-934-5; 688,455 bytes, 49 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

**Book 2:** *Programa de matemàtiques de segona ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 7–10) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 355/2023 (BOPA Núm. 91, 19-7-2023); ISBN 978-99920-0-847-2; 1,808,872 bytes, 56 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Angola's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **6 books, all pupil textbooks: Matemática, Manuais da 1.ª–6.ª Classe (Ministério da Educação — MED, Angola; updated editions of 2021, in Portuguese). There is no teacher guide in the Angola Maths set in hand, so every Angola row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Angola counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 229 teaching items. The expansion map's “6 student + 0 teacher = 6” is correct: download-lists/angola.md, the phase3-staging checklist, textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files all give the same six distinct pupil textbooks, and nothing else. None of the six PDFs was on local disk (pusher-secured only), so all six were fetched fresh for the read from the books' recorded second official source, the ministry's Xilonga host, byte-exact against the records. One set nuance: the portal's 32-manual set contains no 2.ª Classe Matemática at all — the 2.ª book in hand (Book 2) exists only as an unlinked Xilonga file (MAT02-LR.pdf) found by pattern probe on 2026-10-23. The Book 2 and Book 4 PDFs carry a massively duplicated text layer (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the files; the content is fully legible and both books were read in full from it. Twelve book errors are recorded in the placements; they are carried in the book entries below and taught corrected. The placements' named exclusions — the apresentação (ministerial foreword letter) and anthem/flag/civic front matter, the bibliografia pages, pure colouring, tracing and copying practice with no new concept, the editorial production notes printed inside the books, and exercise sets as such (exercises practise the item under which they sit) — are stocked nowhere. **Book 6:** *Matemática — Manual da 6.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 6) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-85-5; 2,544,284 bytes, 104 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book notes, taught corrected: printed p. 51 first presents the ambiguous expression 28 : 7 × 2 with two candidate readings and tells the pupil to ask the teacher — the rule is fixed by the worked examples on printed p. 52, and the course teaches the standard order (× and ÷ in order, before + and −); printed p. 62's direct-proportionality worked line prints the third ratio with denominator 6 where the sequence's term is 5 (150/5 = 30 — taught from the sequence). The book carries printer's proof marks, and one running footer misprints “Matemática 5ª Classe” (printed p. 99) — production matter, cited nowhere.

## Bahamas added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read the Bahamas' whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them Bahamas Ministry of Education curriculum or examination-syllabus documents; 0 pupil (student) textbooks and 0 teacher guides** — and placed 496 requirement items. A curriculum states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Bahamas item in this section is therefore CURRICULUM EVIDENCE — a statement of what the Bahamas requires, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is (Book 3's items are examination-SYLLABUS evidence, on the same standing); the Bahamas is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent, until a Bahamas pupil book is in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded the in-hand set as “4 student + 0 teacher” — the count of 4 is right, the kind is wrong; all four documents are ministry requirement documents (kind = curriculum in both the staging books.json files and textbooks.tsv). The file the records title *BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9* is actually the *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* — a 10-page scanned examination syllabus with no text layer, read visually page by page — and the held Senior High file is the July 2019 revision of the Grades 10–12 guidelines; both are placed as what they are. No pupil textbook or teacher guide is in hand: the pupil books Bahamas schools use are commercial (Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* Books 1–6 — existence verified in the records, no file held; Books 1–2 name Silver Burdett Ginn Mathematics and Harcourt Math in classroom use, and Book 4's bibliography names STP Caribbean Mathematics, Mathematics: A Complete Course, Fundamental Mathematics for the Caribbean and Nelson Caribbean Mathematics — named only, none held), and the one school-compiled pupil text with an exact link in the records (Akhepran International Academy, *Mathematics BJC Textbook — Grade 7*, 2023) was never downloaded and sits in no record set, so it is not part of this read and is cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered objective in each guidelines book's Scope of Work body — the Content column read with every objective — Book 3 at one item per numbered topic row of its Topics table and Book 4 at one item per CONCEPTS/TOPICS block; an objective restated in full on a continuation page is counted once, at its first full statement, and the two requirements the documents restate inside a second strand (Book 1 Grade 2 estimation, item 69; Book 2 Grade 5 rounding of decimals, item 256) are stocked as the placements place them, each row naming the restatement. The placements' named exclusions — Benchmarks, Scope-and-Sequence charts, pacing guides and Essential Questions in Books 1–2; the Problems of the Day sets, Materials Lists, assessment rubrics, the Children's Literature list and References (record items R1–R7 and R9–R11); Book 3's examination administration, its 19 general aims and its erratum sheet (R8); Book 4's Scope and Sequence charts, Glossary and Technology Integration pages — are stocked nowhere. Book errors found in the read are carried corrected on the rows they touch. Into this lesson the placements stock 5 requirement-evidence items from Book 2, Book 3 and Book 4.

**Book 2:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 4–6* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,278,639 bytes, 328 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 54. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 3:** *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* (examination syllabus) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Youth & Sports, Testing & Evaluation Section; 2,351,047 bytes, 10 PDF pages (a scanned-image PDF with no text layer, read visually page by page); printed page = PDF page − 1. The library records title this file “BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9” — the held file is the 2003 examination syllabus, and it is placed as what it is (set correction, stated here; the records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records). SYLLABUS EVIDENCE ONLY — an examination syllabus states what candidates are required to know; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 4:** *Senior High School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 10–12* (curriculum guidelines; the held file is the July 2019 revision, footer “High School Mathematics Curriculum – The Bahamas, 2019”) — Bahamas, Department of Education; 3,766,723 bytes, 219 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

## Belize added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Belize's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **3 documents, all of them Belize Ministry of Education *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide* books (August 2012) — Infant 1, Standard 1 and Standard IV; 0 pupil (student) textbooks** — and placed 108 teaching items. Every document in the set is a teacher's guide: it states the learning outcomes pupils must reach and teaches the teacher how to teach each one (comments, sample lesson objectives, specific content, sample activities and assessment). Every Belize item in this section is therefore TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE — Belize's guides teach teachers how to teach the topic; no Belize pupil textbook is in hand — cited as the evidence kind it is; Belize is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until a Belize pupil book is in hand and read. No pupil Maths book is in hand: the pupil books on the official National Textbook Program list (*My BZ Math* Infant 1–Standard 4, Macmillan; *Caribbean Primary Math* Bk 6 for Standard 5, Hodder; *Active Math* for Standard 6, Macmillan) are all recorded missing in the library files, and the guides' own textbook linkages point to the legacy pupil series *Primary Mathematics for Belize* (Hodder, by term codes Y1T1…/S1T1…), which is named by the guides only — not in hand, not read, and not a course source. Three further Maths documents — the *Primary curriculum: Mathematics* division webs (Lower Division, Middle Division, Upper Division) — are recorded at exact link only, never downloaded and in no books.json; they are not part of this read and are cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered learning outcome in each guide's Standards and Learning Outcomes section (35 outcomes in each guide, 105 in all) plus 3 items taught in a detailed section but stated in no outcome of that guide (items 64, 65 and 87, marked D); outcome numbering is continuous across the series (Infant 1 carries M1.1–M20.1, Standard 1 continues at M1.10, Standard IV at M1.16), which is why the codes do not restart at 1 in the later guides. Printed page = PDF page in all three guides. The placements' named exclusions — the guides' front matter (programme philosophy: goals, rationale, assessment discussion), strand introductions and Big Ideas prose, sample lesson objectives, sample assessments, sample activities and print/technology resource columns as apparatus, the shared manipulatives list, the websites lists, the shared Mathematics Glossary (carried in the placements as record item R2 — a teacher reference list, cited nowhere as content), and the standards each guide marks N/A at its level (Book 1: M2 place value and M13 equations; Book 2: M14 measurement concepts; Book 3: M5 position/ranking numbers and M14) — are stocked nowhere. The 14 book errors and inconsistencies found in the read (placements E1–E14) are carried corrected on the rows they touch: item 35 is placed as data interpretation, not probability, though the guide files it under its probability standard (E5); item 51's set language is taught with “complement” for the book's “compliment” (E6); item 66's many-to-one correspondence is taught as one symbol or unit standing for many objects, not the guide's function-style definition (E8); item 82 is stocked WITHOUT the outcome's fourth bullet (“expand powers with same base to add or subtract”), which is not a valid add/subtract procedure (E9); item 81 is placed as stated and flagged as vague and advanced for its level (E10); item 104 is placed as measurement and taught with the standard measurement meanings, the guide's own definition of precision being a data-management sense (E13); and item 106 follows the guide's own correct definitions — its assessment text's “medium” for median is an error (E12). Into this lesson the placements stock 1 teacher-guide-evidence item from Book 3.

**Book 3:** *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Standard IV* (teacher guide, Grade 6) — Belize, Ministry of Education, August 2012; 868,323 bytes, 51 PDF pages. Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–15; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 16–45. Printed page = PDF page. Outcome numbering continues the series (M1.16 onwards). The detailed sections of this guide are markedly thinner than in Books 1–2 (several state “Specific Content: N/A”); placements rest on the outcomes as stated. TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

## Australia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Australia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **9 books, all of them student textbooks** (Years 8–10: eight Cambridge volumes — the Essential Mathematics Australian Curriculum, Victorian Curriculum and CambridgeMATHS NSW lines, plus the GOLD support edition and the CORE pathway volume — and one Jacaranda volume, Maths Quest 10+10A) — and placed 844 teaching items. Australia has no government textbook series (ACARA writes the Australian Curriculum but publishes and prescribes no textbooks); the set is the school-used commercial series the country records name, and every book is cited as such. All 9 files are distinct works (distinct ISBNs, extents and contents, verified per book on the read); the publisher-line relations are stated in the placements: Book A1 (GOLD) is the support edition of Book A2's Year 8 material, Book A3 is the same line in its Victorian ©2024 edition, Books A4 and A6 are the NSW syllabus editions, and Book A9 is Book A8's CORE pathway companion. In the sections below the books are cited as Books A1–A9. Australia COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure: every item is pupil-textbook teaching. The read's four flagged claims are carried exactly: (F1) Book A3's contents lists its Chapter 11, Algorithmic thinking, as “(online only)”, but the file in hand contains the full chapter, bound after the Answers (printed pp. 926–941) — it is read and placed, and it founds this course's algorithmic-thinking lesson; (F2) Book A7's contents lists a Chapter 18, Algorithmic thinking, with no page numbers, and the chapter is NOT in the file (searched end to end; the book closes with its Glossary, printed p. 1106, and Index, printed p. 1117) — recorded as absent, not read, and no item rests on it; (F3) Book A9's running head at printed p. 644 prints section 10E's code as “0E” — the book's own production error; the placement cites 10E, as the section's heading and contents give it; (F4) only the 9 Maths books are this read's set — the country records' wider Australia picture (12 staged books across subjects, plus filled-by-exact-link titles) is not merged into this count, and in particular the two senior Maths titles that exist only as exact-link records (Cambridge Senior Specialist Mathematics, QCE Units 3&4, 2e, and Cambridge Senior Mathematical Methods, QCE Units 3&4, 2e) are NOT in hand and were NOT read. The placements' named exclusions — front matter and “how to use this book” guides; every chapter's pre-tests, progress quizzes, unlettered applications / problem-solving / modelling / investigation spreads, puzzles and games, chapter summaries, checklists and reviews, semester reviews, glossaries, indexes and answer sections — are apparatus/assessment, teach no new content, and are stocked nowhere. The set is Years 8–10 secondary only, so the primary lessons (counting, place value, first steps) receive no Australia item — that is the set's shape, not a reading gap. Australia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (9/9) has been read and placed; the two senior QCE exact-link titles do not block the in-hand class (if exact-link titles are ever ruled in-scope for the courses reckoning, Australia drops to PARTIAL until they are grabbed and read). Into this lesson the placements stock 20 items from Book A1, Book A2, Book A3, Book A4, Book A5, Book A6.

## Brazil added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Brazil's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDF — **1 book, a pupil textbook: *Matemática — Livro Didático Público — Ensino Médio* (SEED-PR), the Paraná state public textbook for Ensino Médio (Grades 10–12), in Portuguese. There is no teacher guide in the Brazil Maths set in hand, so every Brazil row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Brazil counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 128 teaching items. The expansion map's “1 student + 0 teacher = 1” is correct: books.json (grade-10/gov/brazil) and textbooks.tsv each carry exactly one Brazil Mathematics entry, and phase3-push-state.json lists the file as pushed. The staging folder for the book is empty, so there was no local copy to read, and the authenticated GitHub asset route failed in this pass (the mid-size-books release's asset listing repeatedly disconnected through the proxy, and the asset's download URL answered 404) — the GitHub copy's existence and size were therefore NOT verified here, and a library-side check of that asset is owed (a route failure, not a negative finding). The book was instead read from its own recorded source_url — the Wayback capture banked by the 2026-10-22 CDX prep (capture 20240421222141) — which landed at exactly the prep's expected 6,379,928 B, with 240 pages and the correct cover and catalogue block; the book read is certainly this book. Printed page = PDF page throughout. Four book errors found in the read (placements E1–E4) are carried corrected on the rows they touch — in the sequences-and-series (E1), ratio-and-proportion (E2), coordinate-geometry (E3), barycentres (E4) and loci (E4) lessons; two further misprints are recorded, not taught (R5: the risk exponent typeset once as 0.2525 for 0.2528, printed p. 58 — noted on the item 28 row in the logarithms-and-exponentials lesson; R6: glucose printed “C6A12O6”, printed p. 35, a chemistry misprint), and the Aristarchus bound passage (printed p. 169, record item R7) could not be confirmed either way as extracted and is cited nowhere. The placements' named exclusions — the four section introductions and the general presentation (framing and history), the context essays and debates that carry no new maths, the biographical and historical passages except where the passage itself teaches a method, the “Obras Consultadas” reference lists after every chapter, and the blank “Anotações” pages — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 1 item (all from the pupil textbook) from Book 1.

**Book 1:** *Matemática — Livro Didático Público — Ensino Médio* (pupil textbook, Ensino Médio — Grades 10–12) — Brazil, Secretaria de Estado da Educação do Paraná (SEED-PR), Curitiba, 2nd edition 2006, ISBN 85-85380-39-X; 6,379,928 bytes, 240 PDF pp., MD5 e3dac71a6d075dd142282376da6fa785. Read from the banked Wayback capture of the book's recorded source_url at the exact recorded size; the GitHub release-asset copy could not be verified in this pass (route failure — a library-side asset check is owed). The catalogue block's “p. 216” is the 1st-edition pagination and does not match this file's 240 pages (record item R1); all citations use the actual printed folios. Folha 6's chapter heading (printed p. 77) adds “Corrupção & Política –” to the contents-list title (record item R2) — the same chapter. Folhas 9 and 10 are filed by the book inside its Funções section although their content is trigonometry (record item R3); the placements follow the content.
